# Julius’s T3 Code weekend PRs: relevance to Solus

Reviewed on 19 October 2026. Repository: [pingdotgg/t3code](https://github.com/pingdotgg/t3code). Author: [juliusmarminge](https://github.com/juliusmarminge).

## Implementation follow-up — 19 October 2026

**The user approved the five UI changes and the six work steps after the investigation. Seven Opus 5.5 sessions implemented and reviewed the changes below.** The investigation and all 446 PR rows remain below as a historical record. Their statements about unapproved choices, unchanged source, and compiler activation describe the earlier investigation, not the current checkout.

**Source implementation and the planned automated verification are complete.** The final compiler guard passes after the dependency update and #20785 correction. The full mobile typecheck reports zero errors. Native device and live-provider verification remain pending.

### Performance numbers we can support

These are fixture counts and compile results. They do not measure phone frame rate, typing delay, memory, or end-to-end response time.

| Check                                                           | Before          | After          | Meaning and limit                                                                                                                     |
| --------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------- | -------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Earlier-history mounting model                                  | Up to 28 passes | Up to 2 passes | About 93% fewer passes in the fixture model. This is not a measured React commit count.                                               |
| Thread-list item identities changed on a minute tick            | 31 of 37        | 0 of 37        | The list no longer rebuilds its items for the clock. The 30 rows with snooze menus still update each minute.                          |
| Extra HostFacts notification for an equal refresh               | 1               | 0              | Reads and freshness updates still occur when required.                                                                                |
| Project-array replacements on an equal reload                   | 2               | 0              | Equal project snapshots remain stable.                                                                                                |
| PR notifications on an equal refresh                            | 3               | 2              | Loading and loaded states still notify. The unchanged listing and rows stay stable, and the selector skips walking the listing again. |
| Unrelated settings writes that re-rendered the observed readers | 2 of 2          | 0 of 2         | Measured with focused fixtures.                                                                                                       |
| Known React Compiler skips                                      | 52              | 28             | The final guard compiles 542 of 570 attempted functions across 502 modules. This is coverage, not a measured speed gain.              |

Evidence: [store report](/Users/sidhu/solus/plans/t3-performance-review-2026-10-19/store-stability-implementation.md), [native performance report](/Users/sidhu/solus/plans/t3-performance-review-2026-10-19/native-performance-implementation.md), and [compiler report](/Users/sidhu/solus/plans/t3-performance-review-2026-10-19/measurement-compiler/implementation-report.md).

### Final automated verification

The parent reran `bun run lint:mobile-compiler`: it passes. The worker's final evidence covers both platforms, with zero reported problems, no iOS/Android divergence and all 1,043 source maps correct. The six final test files pass. The one integrated mobile typecheck reports zero errors. The earlier wider run passed 26 of 27 files; the Home project-name disambiguation case also fails on the before snapshot.

### Remaining runtime checks

Run the native and live-provider checks with an approved runtime setup. Record real latency, frame and memory results before making device-speed claims. Check composer wrap and IME behavior, navigation and retained tabs, long-list scroll anchors, native controls and Share, accessibility, and the Agents list. Check Codex late-child completion and Stop in a live run; a synthetic parent wake is not implemented.

The native dependency update needs a rebuild before device verification. The Expo preset now leaves async default parameters intact in the PR diff scroll functions. The pinned Hermes compiler accepts their syntax, but scroll-to-file, scroll-to-top and the release Hermes compiler still need runtime/release checks.

PR-link caching remains deferred because the measured parsing cost did not justify it. Existing failures involving Home project-name disambiguation and an unrelated theme-token edit remain documented separately. No app build, release, commit or pull request is part of this verification.

## Investigation follow-up — completed 19 October 2026

**All 32 open source investigations now have a disposition.** The result is **16 ready references, three covered, four with no separate change to take, three deferred, and six decisions**. The 16 ready references form **six work areas**, not 16 independent optimizations. The original 446-PR inventory remains intact below. Each of the 32 appendix rows and the review ledger now carries the follow-up result.

“Ready” means there is enough local evidence to plan a focused change. It does not mean the change is implemented, a native speed gain is measured, or device verification is complete. No product source, app configuration, dependency, or live data was changed. Analysis scripts and fixtures are kept with this report.

### Confirmed work and order

| Work area                                               | What the investigation established                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | Implementation boundary and proof still required                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| ------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Native compiler, including one correctness prerequisite | The current Expo configuration enables no React compilation. Across 117 selected files, an enabled probe compiled 232 functions and skipped 31 distinct functions, with 76 diagnostics. A direct call to the installed Expo Metro Babel transformer confirmed activation using the real local Babel config. GuideNoticeCard returns before useState on its ready branch; this violates hook order. | Fix the guide hook order first. Add a function-level compiler baseline, then migrate only diagnosed functions in small stages. Keep existing manual caches until the owning function compiles and its lifetime is checked. Native render, typing, scroll and failure-state measurements remain required. |
| Equal host facts                                        | An equal refresh publishes a new ready wrapper and notifies readers of the same fact key. A recovered connection already avoids repeat reads; a fresh session correctly reloads but publishes equal data again.                                                                                                                                                                                    | Group #20392 and #20855. Retain equal bounded values at HostFacts.write, while keeping freshness, error/loading states, access revocation and generation guards. Check desktop, web and native consumers.                                                                                                |
| Native project snapshots                                | One equal ThreadDirectory reload replaces the projects array twice and the equal project wrapper once. Project-only subscribers receive a changed snapshot.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | Preserve unchanged project values, wrappers and sorted arrays. Keep host-state and session changes observable. Cover rename, add/remove, repository change, host relabel and stale responses.                                                                                                            |
| Native PR snapshots and selectors                       | A real PullRequestDirectory equal list refresh emits three notifications and replaces equal PR DTOs. usePullRequestList rebuilds hosts and row wrappers from a directory-wide revision.                                                                                                                                                                                                            | Data sharing alone is insufficient. Preserve equal PR data and separate content snapshots from load state. Keep loading, errors, progressive answers, permissions and check updates. Verify row render counts on native.                                                                                 |
| Project-relative file mutations                         | Synthetic create and delete operations followed an intermediate symlink to a sibling fixture folder. Lexical containment does not enforce the function's stated project boundary.                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | Add resolved-parent containment with explicit final-symlink behavior. Keep intentional general file saves outside the project unchanged. This is a mutation-boundary defect, not a proved authorization bypass.                                                                                          |
| Codex late-child delivery                               | A failed root already exits immediately, so T3's blocked-queue symptom is absent. Root cleanup removes child routing; a later native-child completion is dropped. The retained normalizer can parse that same completion.                                                                                                                                                                          | Keep a bounded owner for live children separate from the active root run. Do not hold the failed root or its queue open. Test failed/successful roots, a new root turn, stop, reconnect, shutdown and duplicate-report prevention.                                                                       |

The first compiler change should be the coverage check and the hook-order correction. The host, project and PR store changes can then be measured independently. Keep file containment and Codex child ownership as separate correctness changes. The previously identified native list-size and Android fixes remain separate candidates; this follow-up does not claim to have reproduced them on a device.

### What the probes proved

The store run passed **33 tests**: five new characterization probes and 28 existing tests. The server/connection baseline passed **78 existing tests**, and the platform run passed **five synthetic probes**. Total: **116 passing tests and probes, zero failures in those final runs**. Four additional isolated Git discovery checks passed. Characterization probes assert current behavior, including confirmed defects; they are evidence, not regression tests for a fix.

The broad compiler check used the installed Babel/Expo preset with a reconstructed Metro caller: **234 serial transforms over 117 selected files**, with no transform crashes. It used Babel 7.29.0, Expo preset 58.0.12 and React Compiler 1.0.0. A separate smoke check called the **installed Expo Metro Babel transformer with the actual local Babel configuration** on SourceFileSurface: compiler off produced zero memo-cache calls; compiler on produced two. Neither check started Metro, bundled the app or ran a native build. Compilation does not measure user-visible performance.

In-memory source experiments showed that narrow changes can make ComposerEditor, FileMarkdownPreview, the PR action hook, StatusFadeIn and the guide notice compile. Larger composer, feed and sidebar experiments exposed additional diagnostics after the first blocker was removed. The report therefore does not prescribe deleting all manual memoization. Keep synchronous-throw and rejected-promise cleanup when changing try/finally. Some ref diagnostics are compiler alias-analysis limits, not proof of an existing React runtime defect.

PR-link parsing took a median **0.112 ms per 100 links** in nine warmed host-only samples (range 0.109–0.125 ms). This is a synthetic Bun microbenchmark, not a Svelte or phone profile. It does not justify a new cache now. Mutation checks also show why a WeakMap keyed only by a mutable link can return stale results.

### Closed leads, deferrals and choices

- **Covered:** #20149 already has the iPad drawer background; #20445's paired route is not replaced by a newly discovered HTTP address; #20835's Claude query retains background-task lifetime and emits a late nested launch with its parent ID. The Claude proof covers the adapter, not every provider timing or UI projection.
- **No separate change:** #20379, #20396 and #20397 are React web compiler ports; their useful native lessons are included in the measured mobile project. #21260 finds no current local hookless-use target: a syntax scan checked 484 native source files and 87 use-prefixed definitions, with zero candidates.
- **Deferred:** #21147 PR-link caching has no measured material cost. #20813's combined Android navigation redesign conflicts with the current two-tab plan; investigate bounded retention only if measured tab returns are slow. #20384's fingerprint defect is reproduced, but the inspected Solus release setup does not use fingerprint-based OTA compatibility. It must be fixed before such a release policy is adopted.
- **Optional UI choices:** #20327 native iOS segmented controls, #20812 compact-until-wrap composer, #21250 native Share, and #21319 close/apply icons. Recommended default: leave these unchanged in the first performance batch. Each has a concrete implementation boundary and device checklist in the investigation register. Current Copy link is already present. A settings X must not promise to undo options that apply immediately.
- **Git policy decision (#20440):** implicit bare discovery is confirmed; an option-injection or command-execution defect was not proved. An explicit-only policy needs a decision about supported bare workflows, followed by compatibility tests.
- **Snapshot contract decision (#20446):** masked input values survive in raw snapshot RPC data, but the ordinary agent text omits element.value and the RPC is owner-gated. This is a data-minimization gap, not evidence of an ordinary password leak into provider text. Recommended small follow-up: omit password/CSS-masked values at extraction. A stronger guarantee for secrets reflected into labels or frames is separate work.

The six decisions do not block the confirmed performance work. No optional redesign is assumed approved by this report.

### Remaining verification before release

No real browser, provider, simulator or physical device was used. No app build or dev server was started. Native iOS and Android still need measurements for render counts, long-list stability, keyboard/IME behavior, focus, navigation and memory. Shared store changes need desktop, web and native checks over IPC and WebSockets. The compiler smoke used an iOS caller; Android transform and native runtime checks remain release gates. Source and synthetic event proofs do not replace these checks.

The baseline is the current working tree at commit `14eec2300a7619ffa21a42b2bf126de38dbd4d47`, including existing uncommitted work. Installed package versions, source lines and upstream status can change. This follow-up examined the 32 open investigations; it did not re-review all 446 patches or all dependency binaries.

```sh
python3 plans/t3-performance-review-2026-10-19/investigation-git-safety.py
```

## What to take from this work

The best performance work to transfer is a **small native mobile program**: add render measurements, test better size estimates for long conversation lists, then enable the React Compiler with checks that prove which files it compiles. Most of the web PRs change React code. Solus desktop and web use Svelte, so those patches are not direct ports.

Solus already has important protections found in this PR series: separate notifications for mobile transcript rows, local composer draft state, segmented transcript calculations in Svelte, virtualized PR lists, and repository-scoped PR refreshes. Preserve these. Do not count a repeated compiler cleanup or an existing protection as another performance gain.

The initial source review found **11 direct candidates, 32 targeted investigations, 23 covered, and 380 with no direct Solus change identified**. The follow-up above resolves all 32 investigation labels; use its decisions and the current totals below. The 11 candidates comprise five performance or measurement items, four correctness items, and two dependency-maintenance items. Related compiler PRs remain one staged project.

The appendix gives every PR its own current decision. “Ready after investigation,” “Deferred,” and “Decision” refer to the follow-up above; original assessments remain in the ledger. “Relevant” means a supported transfer candidate, not a measured speed gain in Solus. “Investigate” means the idea needs local evidence before implementation. “Covered” means the main purpose already has a local equivalent; it does not mean every upstream line was ported. “Not applicable” means there is no direct change to take for this task.

## Scope and method

The exact Saturday–Sunday window is **17–18 October 2026, in UTC**. It contains **404 PRs**. I also reviewed **42 PRs from Friday, 16 October**, because they start the compiler and profiling series. The total is **446 unique PRs**. This is a creation-date inventory, not a count of PRs merged that weekend. The date basis is explicit because GitHub’s date search uses UTC; it is not a Toronto-local weekend query.

[Reproduce the three-day GitHub search](https://github.com/pingdotgg/t3code/pulls?q=is%3Apr+author%3Ajuliusmarminge+created%3A2026-10-16..2026-10-18). Every result page was collected. Each PR body and its code changes were reviewed. The inventory has **1,419 changed-file entries**. Four omitted API patches were recovered from complete PR diffs, including the two large sidebar files. Returned patch line counts and per-PR file counts were checked against GitHub metadata. Lockfile and generated changes were considered as dependency evidence, not as independent performance fixes.

At collection, **438 PRs were merged, six were open, and two were closed without merge**. The appendix preserves that status. The review does not cover every historical revision, review comment, or PR by another author. Binary archive contents were not audited; archive updates were assessed from their version changes and descriptions. The two closed PRs received a separate check of the author’s closing comments. It does not assume that a `perf` title proves a gain.

Solus was compared against the working files in `/Users/sidhu/solus`, with base commit `14eec2300a7619ffa21a42b2bf126de38dbd4d47`. The checkout already had substantial changes in progress, including mobile upstream ports. “Covered” includes those working files and does not claim that they are committed or released. Source line numbers describe the files read during this review and can move as other work continues.

The initial pass was a source review. The follow-up above adds isolated tests, transform checks and a host microbenchmark. No native device run, real browser session, app build or product implementation was performed.

## Recommended performance work

### 1. Measure native renders before changing memoization

**Read:** [#21001 — native render measurement](https://github.com/pingdotgg/t3code/pull/21001). **Priority:** first. **Effort:** M. **Change risk:** medium. **Confidence:** high that the measurement method is relevant; no local gain is yet known.

The upstream tool records components that actually render, with their causes, through the development React inspector. Its driver uses repeated scenarios and reports both commits and renders. This is useful because a compiler can accept a file without making the app faster. Some later T3 compiler PRs report little change in renders per commit.

Solus has the same native screen family: [ThreadDetailScreen](/Users/sidhu/solus/apps/mobile/src/features/threads/ThreadDetailScreen.tsx:105), [ThreadFeed](/Users/sidhu/solus/apps/mobile/src/features/threads/ThreadFeed.tsx:852), and [HomeScreen](/Users/sidhu/solus/apps/mobile/src/features/home/HomeScreen.tsx:240). Adapt the driver to Solus RPC, routes, and disposable fixtures. Keep the counter out of production. Do not import T3’s state or connection system.

Measure typing, an open conversation streaming, another conversation streaming while Home is visible, switching conversations, and loading older mixed-size messages. Use the same base, fixture, device, and interaction for each comparison. Record medians from repeated runs. Include frame time and input response, not only render counts. Native interactive verification is separate follow-up work and was not run for this document.

### 2. Separate long and short messages in native list size estimates

**Read:** [#21269 — prevent a large-history list update loop](https://github.com/pingdotgg/t3code/pull/21269). **Priority:** high. **Effort:** S–M. **Change risk:** medium. **Confidence:** high for the matching source pattern; the failure has not been reproduced in Solus native.

T3’s list grouped all assistant messages under one size estimate. Very tall replies raised the average, so short unmeasured replies started with very large estimated heights. Measuring one row could expose the next row repeatedly. The patch groups message types by text-length ranges and adds a test with short replies above long ones.

Solus native still uses `message:${row.role}` at [ThreadFeed.tsx:877](/Users/sidhu/solus/apps/mobile/src/features/threads/ThreadFeed.tsx:877), with an estimated item size of 180 at line 885. It uses `@legendapp/list` 3.3.5. This is a direct pattern match. T3 reproduced the problem in its React web list; Solus uses the native list, so confirm the native size-estimation behavior before declaring the same crash.

Adapt the type calculation to Solus’s message model. Keep its per-item subscription boundary: calculating a size class must not make the whole feed read every message’s streamed content. Test short and very long messages, empty messages, streamed growth, history prepends, expanded tool groups, images, width changes, and keyboard changes. The visible anchor must remain stable and the list must settle. Desktop and web use a different transcript virtualizer; do not copy the native list patch into Svelte.

### 3. Enable the mobile compiler with a shrinking exception list

**Start with:** [#20967 — Metro compiler setup and coverage checks](https://github.com/pingdotgg/t3code/pull/20967). **Priority:** after the baseline. **Effort:** L for the staged program. **Change risk:** medium–high. **Confidence:** high that the setup is missing; local performance effect is unmeasured.

Solus’s [Expo configuration](/Users/sidhu/solus/apps/mobile/app.json:2) has no compiler experiment, and [babel.config.js](/Users/sidhu/solus/apps/mobile/babel.config.js:1) only names the Expo preset. Verify the actual Metro transform first. The upstream change tests the transform that Metro uses and records compiler skips, including silent skips. Its exception list must shrink when files become supported.

Treat the follow-up PRs as one migration: [#20969](https://github.com/pingdotgg/t3code/pull/20969), [#20970](https://github.com/pingdotgg/t3code/pull/20970), [#20971](https://github.com/pingdotgg/t3code/pull/20971), [#21119](https://github.com/pingdotgg/t3code/pull/21119), [#21124](https://github.com/pingdotgg/t3code/pull/21124), [#21133](https://github.com/pingdotgg/t3code/pull/21133), [#21136](https://github.com/pingdotgg/t3code/pull/21136), [#21137](https://github.com/pingdotgg/t3code/pull/21137), [#21141](https://github.com/pingdotgg/t3code/pull/21141), [#21142](https://github.com/pingdotgg/t3code/pull/21142), [#21145](https://github.com/pingdotgg/t3code/pull/21145), and [#21268](https://github.com/pingdotgg/t3code/pull/21268). Only adopt screen changes where Solus has the same code and need. T3-only usage, device, and account screens do not create new Solus requirements.

Keep manual memoization until the relevant local files demonstrably compile and their behavior is checked. Follow [#21255](https://github.com/pingdotgg/t3code/pull/21255) as a warning about callback identity after compilation. Solus already uses explicit callbacks in [HomeScreen](/Users/sidhu/solus/apps/mobile/src/features/home/HomeScreen.tsx:242); the upstream callback defect is not established here. Include focus, expiry, route changes, and asynchronous cleanup in the tests. Compiler support can change how often code runs, which can expose values that previously depended on incidental renders.

### 4. Equal refreshes — confirmed by the follow-up

**Read:** [#20392](https://github.com/pingdotgg/t3code/pull/20392), [#20821](https://github.com/pingdotgg/t3code/pull/20821), [#20850](https://github.com/pingdotgg/t3code/pull/20850), and [#20855](https://github.com/pingdotgg/t3code/pull/20855). **Priority:** confirmed work; see the follow-up for the measured boundary. **Effort:** M. **Change risk:** medium. **Confidence:** high for extra identity changes; render savings remain unmeasured.

The transferable rule is to preserve values that did not change and subscribe only to fields a reader needs. Solus already partitions host facts by key in [Host](/Users/sidhu/solus/packages/workspace-ui/src/contexts/hosts/host.svelte.ts:43). However, [HostFacts.write](/Users/sidhu/solus/packages/client-core/src/host-facts.ts:250) always publishes a new fact, and PR record updates assign nested values such as labels and capabilities in [PullRequest.apply](/Users/sidhu/solus/packages/workspace-ui/src/contexts/prs/pull-request.svelte.ts:193).

The follow-up measured equal host, native project and native PR refreshes and confirmed extra identity changes. Svelte PullRequest.apply was not included in those probes. A confirmed change should preserve equal data while still publishing loading, errors, access changes, freshness, and real updates. Avoid a generic deep-comparison pass over every streamed payload. A Svelte field update already has different invalidation behavior from a React context update.

### 5. Defer PR-link caching; keep clock work separate

**Read:** [#21147](https://github.com/pingdotgg/t3code/pull/21147), [#20814](https://github.com/pingdotgg/t3code/pull/20814), and [#20819](https://github.com/pingdotgg/t3code/pull/20819). **Priority:** lower, measure first. **Effort:** S–M. **Change risk:** medium. **Confidence:** medium.

[linkedPrIdentity](/Users/sidhu/solus/packages/workspace-ui/src/contexts/prs/linked-pr.ts:20) parses a link URL each time. The follow-up host probe found about 0.112 ms per 100 links, so caching is deferred unless a renderer profile shows material cost. T3’s cache relies on immutable link objects. Solus can mutate reactive objects, so a blind object-keyed cache can become stale. Prefer normalization at a suitable owner or a cache that also checks the URL and scope inputs, if profiling justifies one.

[HomeScreen](/Users/sidhu/solus/apps/mobile/src/features/home/HomeScreen.tsx:280) updates a minute clock and separately schedules snooze wakeups. The Svelte sidebar also has a retention clock in [SessionSidebar](/Users/sidhu/solus/packages/workspace-ui/src/components/session/SessionSidebar.svelte:277). Check which calculations and rows each tick changes. Move label-only work to the label, but preserve exact snooze and retention behavior. Do not remove a clock because a label happens to look unchanged in one capture.

Another small measurement target is [#20848](https://github.com/pingdotgg/t3code/pull/20848): mobile [AgentDefaultsScreen](/Users/sidhu/solus/apps/mobile/src/features/settings/AgentDefaultsScreen.tsx:31) and [NotificationSettingsScreen](/Users/sidhu/solus/apps/mobile/src/features/settings/NotificationSettingsScreen.tsx:34) read the full personal settings snapshot. Check whether an unrelated setting change rebuilds these screens before adding selected-field subscriptions. This is a settings-page concern, not evidence that typing rebuilds the conversation.

Two dependency PRs are also useful maintenance references. [#20389](https://github.com/pingdotgg/t3code/pull/20389) updates Expo patch versions and removes upstreamed native patches. Solus’s [manifest](/Users/sidhu/solus/apps/mobile/package.json:33) and [lockfile](/Users/sidhu/solus/bun.lock:2265) still resolve older versions for several affected packages. Audit versions and patches together; do not remove a patch just because T3 removed its copy. [#20400](https://github.com/pingdotgg/t3code/pull/20400) updates Enriched Markdown to `t3.15`, while [Solus pins `t3.11`](/Users/sidhu/solus/apps/mobile/package.json:60). Its wrap, list, and Android selection fixes are relevant to the same native renderer. The binary archive contents were not examined, so review the intervening source and native compatibility before updating. Neither dependency PR establishes a Solus speed gain.

## Work already covered, or not worth copying

- **Streaming isolation:** [TranscriptTurns](/Users/sidhu/solus/packages/workspace-ui/src/components/conversation/lib/transcript-turns.svelte.ts:16) calculates segments separately. [ConversationStore](/Users/sidhu/solus/apps/mobile/src/features/conversation/conversation-store.ts:60) separates row, order, and screen-state notifications. These already implement much of the intent behind the stream-subscription series.
- **Draft isolation:** [ThreadComposer](/Users/sidhu/solus/apps/mobile/src/features/threads/ThreadComposer.tsx:266) owns its draft state. The main purpose of [#21261](https://github.com/pingdotgg/t3code/pull/21261) is already present. This still deserves a regression measurement, not a replacement with T3 atoms.
- **PR request control:** [PrSync.turnEnded](/Users/sidhu/solus/packages/server/src/prs/pr-sync.ts:388) checks whether Git moved and publishes for that repository. [PrIndex.listPages](/Users/sidhu/solus/packages/server/src/prs/pr-index.ts:212) shares cached batched reads. [#20093](https://github.com/pingdotgg/t3code/pull/20093) is useful audit material, but its global-invalidation defect is not established in Solus.
- **React web compiler conversions:** do not apply React hooks, React Compiler lint rules, or T3’s Atom/Effect runtime to Svelte. The broad compiler cleanup is useful background for the native pilot, not hundreds of independent Solus improvements.
- **Bulk browser-test migration:** use the intent of [#20797](https://github.com/pingdotgg/t3code/pull/20797) and its follow-ups when a real browser behavior needs proof. Do not migrate all Solus tests because T3 changed its React test runner. Native tests need a native measurement path.
- **Closed PRs with no measured gain:** [#20844’s closing comment](https://github.com/pingdotgg/t3code/pull/20844#issuecomment-6232876285) identifies development-server restarts as the source of misleading render counts. [#20961’s closing comment](https://github.com/pingdotgg/t3code/pull/20961#issuecomment-6238106401) reports no improvement and closes the change. Neither is a shipped optimization to port.
- **Unsettled mobile measurement:** [#20963](https://github.com/pingdotgg/t3code/pull/20963) is open and its current body reports conflicting historical measurements. Its subscription idea can be studied; its original speed claim should not be repeated as established.

## Order, surfaces, and proof

Start with the native measurement baseline. Test the list-estimate change independently. Then add the compiler setup and a small set of screens; expand only after the coverage check and native scenarios pass. Keep the Android correctness ports separate from the compiler work. The follow-up confirms three store work areas. Keep their render measurements as acceptance checks; PR-link caching remains deferred. Clock work remains a separate candidate.

For follow-up implementation, use focused existing tests such as `bun test tests/unit/native-mobile-conversation.test.ts tests/unit/native-mobile-thread-list.test.ts`, `bun test tests/unit/transcript-turns.test.ts`, `bun test tests/unit/host-facts.test.ts`, and `bun test tests/unit/pr-sync.test.ts tests/unit/github-pr-search.test.ts` as appropriate. Native type checks use `bun run --cwd apps/mobile check`. These were suggested checks in the first pass. The follow-up above records the tests actually run; unlisted checks remain unrun. Do not run the prohibited full build as an audit step. Source or unit checks alone do not prove device render speed or scrolling stability.

The compiler and Android changes apply to their native surfaces. Shared store changes must be checked in desktop, web, and mobile. Preserve IPC and WebSocket behavior, reconnects, multiple hosts, and concurrent mounted conversations. Streaming and agent-roster tests must cover both Claude and Codex where the behavior applies. No new RPC feature or provider capability is proposed by the performance shortlist. No client capability is removed.

Session settings has Cancel and Save/Done text buttons. The existing toolbar already supports prominent buttons, so this needs no new native module. Crucial semantic limit: only a pending model and its pending options are staged; changing reasoning/fast mode for the current model or permission mode can apply immediately. An X must not be described as undoing all settings. ## Evidence and limits

The accompanying [review ledger](/Users/sidhu/solus/plans/t3-performance-review-2026-10-19/review-ledger.json) keeps the full per-PR assessment, confidence, surface scope, and all local references. The [source inventory](/Users/sidhu/solus/plans/t3-performance-review-2026-10-19/source-inventory.json) keeps the query, dates, upstream commit IDs, file names, and evidence hashes. These make the 446-entry coverage check repeatable. A [Markdown copy](/Users/sidhu/solus/plans/t3-performance-review-2026-10-19/report.md) is also available. The document is an assessment of transfer value, not an implementation plan or a guarantee that an upstream patch applies cleanly.
